Oh, goodness, no, it's nothing quite like that. I mean, I certainly haven't forgotten how to make my tea or... or the names of my friends. That would be dreadful, wouldn't it?

Well, I... I suppose I have been a little less active lately. But I think that's perfectly normal, isn't it? When you're not feeling quite as sharp, you just naturally slow down a bit. 

I mean, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be a good patient for you—I haven't really been keeping up with my hobbies. Things I used to just love, like my watercolors, or tending to my tomato plants... even the line dancing I used to do, and watching the cardinals out back. They just sort of sit there now. I look at them and I just... I can't quite make myself care about them the way I used to. 

But I'm sure it's just a phase, or maybe I'm just a bit tired. I don't want you to think I'm depressed or anything unpleasant like that. It's just a little slump, I'm sure of it. Is it alright that I mentioned that? I hope it's helpful for your notes.

Oh, well... let me think. I suppose it's been... maybe about three months now? That this sort of low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around.

I do try my best to keep a cheerful face on things. I hope I don't sound like I'm complaining too much, Doctor. I know you must see people with much worse troubles than a little three-month slump. Well, I... I suppose I have been a little more tired than usual. But at my age, you know, you can't expect to have the energy of a teenager! 

Though, if I'm being perfectly honest with you—and I do want to give you all the right information—it has been a little frustrating. Even when I get a full night's sleep, I... I wake up feeling just as tired. And then, well, by the time I've washed up and made myself a little breakfast, I feel like I just need to sit right back down again. 

It's silly, really. I shouldn't be making a fuss over being a bit worn out. I'm sure it's nothing to worry about, just part of getting older, right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, Doctor. I really do try to push through it when I can.

Oh, well... I suppose I have been moving a bit slower. But again, that's just part of being 79, isn't it? You can't expect me to be sprinting around the house! 

Though... my husband, bless his heart, he did mention something the other day. He... he said I move like I'm wading through mud now. I'm sure it's nothing serious, just a little stiffness or being tired, like we talked about. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing on purpose. Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. 

If anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. 

But, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.

Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. 

I know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.

Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. 

I certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I hope that's the right attitude to have.

Oh, for the past two weeks? No, dear, I really don't think so. I know you just asked about my mood, and I... I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression.

I really do try to push through it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or perhaps feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been moving a bit slower. But again, that's just part of being 79, isn't it? You can't expect me to be sprinting around the house! \n\nThough... my husband, bless his heart, he did mention something the other day. He... he said I move like I'm wading through mud now. I told him he was just being dramatic, but... well, if I'm being perfectly truthful with you, Doctor, I do find that even simple things, like getting up to answer the phone or sort the mail, just... they take me such a long time now. It's like my body just doesn't want to cooperate. \n\nI'm sure it's nothing serious, just a little stiffness or being tired, like we talked about. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing on purpose. I really do try to keep moving when I can. Is that the kind of thing you needed to know? I hope I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh! Goodness, no. No, no, no. I... I know people get down in the dumps, and I suppose I've been feeling a bit low, but I would never... I don't think like that at all. \n\nLife is... well, it's a gift, isn't it? Even when things are a little slow or I'm feeling tired, I still have my husband, and my garden, even if I haven't been out there as much. I would never want to do anything to upset my family or... or be a burden in that way. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would... well, you know. I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I would never want to do anything to upset my family or... or be a burden in that way. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would... well, you know. I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... oh, I suppose I haven't been doing as much of that lately. But it's not that I don't care, exactly! It's just... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic. 

You see, I used to just love getting out my watercolors, and tending to my tomato plants, and... oh, even the line dancing I used to do. And watching the cardinals out back, they're so beautiful. But lately, they just sort of... sit there. The paints, I mean. And I look out the window and I just can't seem to make myself feel that same spark about it all. 

I feel awful even saying it, because I know I should be keeping busy and enjoying my hobbies. It makes me sound so ungrateful, doesn't it? I really am trying to find the joy in things, Doctor, I promise. It's just been a little harder to muster up the energy to care. That's normal though, right? Just a little phase I need to push through?

I suppose it's been... maybe about three months now? That this sort of low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around. \n\nBut really, three months isn't such a long time in the grand scheme of things, is it? I'm sure it's just the change of seasons or maybe I just need to get out a bit more. I do try my best to keep a cheerful face on things. I hope I don't sound like I'm complaining too much, Doctor. Does that timeline make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or fatigued lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little more tired than usual. But at my age, you know, you can't expect to have the energy of a teenager! \n\nThough, if I'm being perfectly honest with you—and I do want to give you all the right information—it has been a little frustrating. Even when I get a full night's sleep, I... I wake up feeling just as tired. And then, well, by the time I've washed up and made myself a little breakfast, I feel like I just need to sit right back down again. \n\nIt's silly, really. I shouldn't be making a fuss over being a bit worn out. I'm sure it's nothing to worry about, just part of getting older, right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, Doctor. I really do try to push through it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or perhaps feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been moving a bit slower. But again, that's just part of being 79, isn't it? You can't expect me to be sprinting around the house! \n\nThough... my husband, bless his heart, he did mention something the other day. He... he said I move like I'm wading through mud now. I told him he was just being dramatic, but... well, if I'm being perfectly truthful with you, Doctor, I do find that even simple things, like getting up to answer the phone or sort the mail, just... they take me such a long time now. It's like my body just doesn't want to cooperate. \n\nI'm sure it's nothing serious, just a little stiffness or being tired, like we talked about. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing on purpose. I really do try to keep moving when I can. Is that the kind of thing you needed to know? I hope I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh! Goodness, no. No, no, no. I... I know people get down in the dumps, and I suppose I've been feeling a bit low, but I would never... I don't think like that at all. \n\nLife is... well, it's a gift, isn't it? Even when things are a little slow or I'm feeling tired, I still have my husband, and my garden, even if I haven't been out there as much. I would never want to do anything to upset my family or... or be a burden in that way. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would... well, you know. I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? It's really nothing terrible, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, actually, that's sort of what brought me in to see you. My head has just been feeling so foggy. I've been getting a bit muddled with simple things, like... oh, mixing up the times for my appointments, or getting the dates wrong for the exhibits I like to go to. And the other day I couldn't remember which of the volunteers I had already called for the church bake sale. It's so silly of me! \n\nI feel dreadful even mentioning it, because I know you have patients with real problems, and here I am fussing over a little forgetfulness. I really do try to keep everything straight, I write things down and everything. It's just... it's been a bit harder lately to keep it all clear in my mind. I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this. You understand what I mean, though, don't you, Doctor? It's just a little foggy patch, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. \n\nI certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I hope that's the right attitude to have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, for the past two weeks? Like I said, it's really just the fogginess that's been bothering me. \n\nI try very hard to be cheerful, I really do. You don't think I sound terribly down, do you, Doctor? I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? It's really nothing terrible, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, actually, that's sort of what brought me in to see you. My head has just been feeling so foggy. I've been getting a bit muddled with simple things, like... oh, mixing up the times for my appointments, or getting the dates wrong for the exhibits I like to go to. And the other day I couldn't remember which of the volunteers I had already called for the church bake sale. It's so silly of me! \n\nI feel dreadful even mentioning it, because I know you have patients with real problems, and here I am fussing over a little forgetfulness. I really do try to keep everything straight, I write things down and everything. It's just... it's been a bit harder lately to keep it all clear in my mind. I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this. You understand what I mean, though, don't you, Doctor? It's just a little foggy patch, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. \n\nI certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I hope that's the right attitude to have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, for the past two weeks? Like I said, it's really just the fogginess that's been bothering me. \n\nI try very hard to be cheerful, I really do. You don't think I sound terribly down, do you, Doctor? Well... I suppose when you put it that way, yes. Goodness knows I have plenty of time on my hands these days. But... well, like I mentioned earlier, the things I always used to love—my watercolors, the tomato plants, the line dancing, and even looking out for the cardinals... they really do just sit there now. 

I look at my paints and I tell myself, 'You should go do that, it'll be good for you,' but... I just can't seem to make myself care about them. Not even a little bit. And yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, I suppose it has been like that almost every day for the last few weeks. 

I feel so terribly lazy admitting that to you. I really shouldn't be wasting my days like this, should I? I know I need to try harder to keep busy. I'm trying to be better about it, Doctor, I truly am. Is it... is it awful of me to just not have the interest right now?

Just the other day I couldn't even remember which volunteer I had already called on the phone. \n\nI just wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by coming in... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these memory issues? For example, do you often forget things that you would normally remember, like familiar names, recent conversations, or how to do everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, it's nothing quite like that. I mean, I certainly haven't forgotten how to make my tea or... or the names of my friends. That would be dreadful, wouldn't it? \n\nIt's really just... well, like I said, just little mix-ups. Maybe I'll have a lovely chat with someone at the gallery and then, a few hours later, I might struggle to recall exactly what we agreed on for the next meeting. But I think everyone does that sometimes, don't they? Especially when there's a lot going on. I haven't forgotten anything truly important, I promise. I just... I want to make sure I'm not making a fuss over normal aging. Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your thinking or memory besides these mix-ups? For example, feeling confused, getting lost in familiar places, or having trouble solving problems?\n\nPatient: Oh, heavens no. Getting lost? No, no, I know my way around the neighborhood and the gallery just fine. I haven't wandered off or anything like that. \n\nAnd as for solving problems... well, I still manage my own checkbook, though I suppose it takes me a tiny bit longer to balance it these days. But I always get there in the end! I certainly don't feel confused in any grand sense. It's really just those silly little scheduling mix-ups I mentioned. \n\nI hope I'm giving you the right sort of answers, Doctor. I just don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything dreadful like that. It's all quite manageable, really. Does that sound like the sort of thing you usually hear from someone my age?\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you found yourself feeling down, sad, or losing interest in activities that you usually enjoy, even just for a little while?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little less active lately. But I think that's perfectly normal, isn't it? When you're not feeling quite as sharp, you just naturally slow down a bit. \n\nI mean, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be a good patient for you—I haven't really been keeping up with my hobbies. Things I used to just love, like my watercolors, or tending to my tomato plants... even the line dancing I used to do, and watching the cardinals out back. They just sort of sit there now. I look at them and I just... I can't quite make myself care about them the way I used to. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a phase, or maybe I'm just a bit tired. I don't want you to think I'm depressed or anything unpleasant like that. It's just a little slump, I'm sure of it. Is it alright that I mentioned that? I hope it's helpful for your notes.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt less interested in your hobbies and activities, like your watercolors or tending to your tomato plants?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... let me think. I suppose it's been... maybe about three months now? That this sort of low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around. \n\nBut really, three months isn't such a long time in the grand scheme of things, is it? I'm sure it's just the change of seasons or maybe I just need to get out a bit more. I do try my best to keep a cheerful face on things. I hope I don't sound like I'm complaining too much, Doctor. I know you must see people with much worse troubles than a little three-month slump. Does that timeline make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or fatigued lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little more tired than usual. But at my age, you know, you can't expect to have the energy of a teenager! \n\nThough, if I'm being perfectly honest with you—and I do want to give you all the right information—it has been a little frustrating. Even when I get a full night's sleep, I... I wake up feeling just as tired. And then, well, by the time I've washed up and made myself a little breakfast, I feel like I just need to sit right back down again. \n\nIt's silly, really. I shouldn't be making a fuss over being a bit worn out. I'm sure it's nothing to worry about, just part of getting older, right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, Doctor. I really do try to push through it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or perhaps feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been moving a bit slower. But again, that's just part of being 79, isn't it? You can't expect me to be sprinting around the house! \n\nThough... my husband, bless his heart, he did mention something the other day. He... he said I move like I'm wading through mud now. I told him he was just being dramatic, but... well, if I'm being perfectly truthful with you, Doctor, I do find that even simple things, like getting up to answer the phone or sort the mail, just... they take me such a long time now. It's like my body just doesn't want to cooperate. \n\nI'm sure it's nothing serious, just a little stiffness or being tired, like we talked about. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing on purpose. I really do try to keep moving when I can. Is that the kind of thing you needed to know? I hope I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh! Goodness, no. No, no, no. I... I know people get down in the dumps, and I suppose I've been feeling a bit low, but I would never... I don't think like that at all. \n\nLife is... well, it's a gift, isn't it? Even when things are a little slow or I'm feeling tired, I still have my husband, and my garden, even if I haven't been out there as much. I would never want to do anything to upset my family or... or be a burden in that way. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would... well, you know. I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? It's really nothing terrible, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, actually, that's sort of what brought me in to see you. My head has just been feeling so foggy. I've been getting a bit muddled with simple things, like... oh, mixing up the times for my appointments, or getting the dates wrong for the exhibits I like to go to. And the other day I couldn't remember which of the volunteers I had already called for the church bake sale. I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this. You understand what I mean, though, don't you, Doctor? It's just a little foggy patch, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. \n\nI certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm not trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been especially low or worse in the mornings lately?\n\nPatient: Worse in the mornings? Oh, goodness, no, I don't believe so. I always try very hard to start the day off on the right foot, you know? \n\nI make sure to get up, make my bed right away, and open the curtains to let the light in. I certainly wouldn't want to just lie there feeling sorry for myself—that sets such a bad tone for the whole day, don't you agree? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the mornings are worse at all. I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you manage to do them?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I suppose I haven't been doing as much of that lately. But it's not that I don't care, exactly! It's just... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic. \n\nYou see, I used to just love getting out my watercolors, and tending to my tomato plants, and... oh, even the line dancing I used to do. And watching the cardinals out back, they're so beautiful. But lately, they just sort of... sit there. The paints, I mean. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... yes, I suppose we did just touch on that, didn't we? I hope I'm not making you repeat yourself because I didn't explain it well enough the first time!

I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? It's really nothing terrible, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, actually, that's sort of what brought me in to see you. My head has just been feeling so foggy. I've been getting a bit muddled with simple things, like... oh, mixing up the times for my appointments, or getting the dates wrong for the exhibits I like to go to. And the other day I couldn't remember which of the volunteers I had already called for the church bake sale. It's so silly of me! \n\nI feel dreadful even mentioning it, because I know you have patients with real problems, and here I am fussing over a little forgetfulness. I really do try to keep everything straight, I write things down and everything. It's just... it's been a bit harder lately to keep it all clear in my mind. I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this. You understand what I mean, though, don't you, Doctor? It's just a little foggy patch, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. \n\nI certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I hope that's the right attitude to have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, for the past two weeks? No, dear, I really don't think so. I know you just asked about my mood, and I... I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression. \n\nI certainly haven't been moping about for weeks on end. That sounds so... well, so gloomy, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to be a bother to anyone by being down in the dumps all the time. I still get up, make my tea, and try to keep busy. Like I said, it's really just the fogginess that's been bothering me. \n\nI try very hard to be cheerful, I really do. You don't think I sound terribly down, do you, Doctor? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm not trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been especially low or worse in the mornings lately?\n\nPatient: Worse in the mornings? Oh, goodness, no, I don't believe so. I always try very hard to start the day off on the right foot, you know? \n\nI make sure to get up, make my bed right away, and open the curtains to let the light in. I think mornings are actually quite nice. I certainly wouldn't want to just lie there feeling sorry for myself—that sets such a bad tone for the whole day, don't you agree? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the mornings are worse at all. I'm doing my absolute best to keep a proper routine and stay productive right from the start. That's the right way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you manage to do them?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I suppose I haven't been doing as much of that lately. But it's not that I don't care, exactly! It's just... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic. \n\nYou see, I used to just love getting out my watercolors, and tending to my tomato plants, and... oh, even the line dancing I used to do. And watching the cardinals out back, they're so beautiful. But lately, they just sort of... sit there. The paints, I mean. And I look out the window and I just can't seem to make myself feel that same spark about it all. \n\nI feel awful even saying it, because I know I should be keeping busy and enjoying my hobbies. It makes me sound so ungrateful, doesn't it? I really am trying to find the joy in things, Doctor, I promise. It's just been a little harder to muster up the energy to care. That's normal though, right? Just a little phase I need to push through?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood has felt low for most of the time, and it didn’t seem to get better, even a little, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, for two weeks straight? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I have my moments where I feel a bit tired or... or flat, like I mentioned with the painting, but it certainly hasn't been an everyday thing, all day long. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around letting myself go. I try very hard to shake it off if I feel a little slump coming on. I'll make a cup of tea or call my neighbor. I wouldn't let it drag on for weeks without trying to fix it. That wouldn't be very responsible of me, would it? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say it's been a constant low like that. I'm doing my best to keep a brave face and keep moving forward. I hope that's the right way to look at it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve completely lost interest in doing your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you have the time and opportunity to do so, and that this has been happening nearly every day for the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Well... I just can't seem to make myself care about them. Not even a little bit. Things I used to just love, like my watercolors, or tending to my tomato plants... even the line dancing I used to do, and watching the cardinals out back. They just sort of sit there now. I look at them and I just... I can't quite make myself care about them the way I used to. \n\nBut I'm sure it's just a phase, or maybe I'm just a bit tired. I don't want you to think I'm depressed or anything unpleasant like that. It's just a little slump, I'm sure of it. Is it alright that I mentioned that? I hope it's helpful for your notes.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt less interested in your hobbies and activities, like your watercolors or tending to your tomato plants?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... let me think. I suppose it's been... maybe about three months now? That this sort of low, slowed-down feeling has been hanging around. \n\nBut really, three months isn't such a long time in the grand scheme of things, is it? I'm sure it's just the change of seasons or maybe I just need to get out a bit more. I do try my best to keep a cheerful face on things. I hope I don't sound like I'm complaining too much, Doctor. I know you must see people with much worse troubles than a little three-month slump. Does that timeline make sense to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or fatigued lately, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I have been a little more tired than usual. But at my age, you know, you can't expect to have the energy of a teenager! \n\nThough, if I'm being perfectly honest with you—and I do want to give you all the right information—it has been a little frustrating. Even when I get a full night's sleep, I... I wake up feeling just as tired. And then, well, by the time I've washed up and made myself a little breakfast, I feel like I just need to sit right back down again. \n\nIt's silly, really. I shouldn't be making a fuss over being a bit worn out. I'm sure it's nothing to worry about, just part of getting older, right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, Doctor. I really do try to push through it when I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or perhaps feeling restless and unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I have been moving a bit slower. But again, that's just part of being 79, isn't it? You can't expect me to be sprinting around the house! \n\nThough... my husband, bless his heart, he did mention something the other day. He... he said I move like I'm wading through mud now. I told him he was just being dramatic, but... well, if I'm being perfectly truthful with you, Doctor, I do find that even simple things, like getting up to answer the phone or sort the mail, just... they take me such a long time now. It's like my body just doesn't want to cooperate. \n\nI'm sure it's nothing serious, just a little stiffness or being tired, like we talked about. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing on purpose. I really do try to keep moving when I can. Is that the kind of thing you needed to know? I hope I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh! Goodness, no. No, no, no. I... I know people get down in the dumps, and I suppose I've been feeling a bit low, but I would never... I don't think like that at all. \n\nLife is... well, it's a gift, isn't it? Even when things are a little slow or I'm feeling tired, I still have my husband, and my garden, even if I haven't been out there as much. I would never want to do anything to upset my family or... or be a burden in that way. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would... well, you know. I'm just a little worn out lately, that's all. I try very hard to focus on the positive things. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more anxious lately, or perhaps fidgety, unable to relax even when you're not doing anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Oh, anxious? Well... no, I wouldn't say I've been anxious, exactly. I mean, everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Especially with the news these days, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling fidgety or anything like that. \n\nIf anything, like I was saying, it's quite the opposite! I've been so slow and tired, I haven't really had the energy to be restless. When I sit down in my chair, I'm usually quite happy just to stay there. I... I hope that's alright to say. I'm not trying to be difficult, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are. I'm doing okay with these questions, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately, or found yourself losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my appetite. I suppose it has been a little off, but you know, when you get to be my age, you just don't need those big, heavy meals anymore, do you? Most days I really just... well, I pick at a little piece of toast or maybe have a small bowl of soup. Food just hasn't been sounding very good to me lately. I really do try to eat, though, I promise! I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself. \n\nBut, um... I suppose I have lost a tiny bit of weight. Not on purpose, mind you! I haven't been dieting or doing anything silly like that. It's just... I put on one of my skirts the other day and noticed it had gotten rather loose around the waist. I'm sure it's nothing to fuss over, though. I am making sure to get something down every day, even if it's just a few bites. That's the right thing to do, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been waking up earlier than usual and having trouble going back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, waking up early? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I've actually been sleeping quite soundly, to tell you the truth. Once I'm in bed, I usually stay asleep right through until my normal time. I certainly don't lie there tossing and turning or staring at the clock in the dark. \n\nI know a lot of folks my age complain about their sleep, but I've been very lucky that way. I just stay tucked in until it's a proper time to get up. Getting a good night's rest is the best thing for you, isn't it, Doctor? I hope you're pleased to hear that part is working just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to concentrate on things, like reading the newspaper or following a TV show, even a little more than before?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... my concentration. I suppose it has been a little... fuzzy lately. But you know, at my age, you expect a little bit of that, don't you? It's really nothing terrible, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, actually, that's sort of what brought me in to see you. My head has just been feeling so foggy. I've been getting a bit muddled with simple things, like... oh, mixing up the times for my appointments, or getting the dates wrong for the exhibits I like to go to. And the other day I couldn't remember which of the volunteers I had already called for the church bake sale. It's so silly of me! \n\nI feel dreadful even mentioning it, because I know you have patients with real problems, and here I am fussing over a little forgetfulness. I really do try to keep everything straight, I write things down and everything. It's just... it's been a bit harder lately to keep it all clear in my mind. I hope I'm not wasting your time with all this. You understand what I mean, though, don't you, Doctor? It's just a little foggy patch, I'm sure of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been consistently low most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Oh, my mood? Well... I wouldn't say it's been consistently low, no. I mean, we all have our days where we feel a little blue, don't we? Especially when the weather is dreary or things are just a bit quiet around the house. \n\nI certainly don't sit around feeling sorry for myself all day, I promise you that! I try to keep my chin up and look on the bright side. It might just be that I've been a little tired with the foggy head and all. But I'm not... I'm not depressed or anything dreadful like that. I always try to put on a smile for people. That's the best way to handle things, isn't it? Just keep smiling and pushing through? I hope that's the right attitude to have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, for the past two weeks? No, dear, I really don't think so. I know you just asked about my mood, and I... I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression. \n\nI certainly haven't been moping about for weeks on end. That sounds so... well, so gloomy, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to be a bother to anyone by being down in the dumps all the time. I still get up, make my tea, and try to keep busy. Like I said, it's really just the fogginess that's been bothering me. \n\nI try very hard to be cheerful, I really do. You don't think I sound terribly down, do you, Doctor? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm not trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been especially low or worse in the mornings lately?\n\nPatient: Worse in the mornings? Oh, goodness, no, I don't believe so. I always try very hard to start the day off on the right foot, you know? \n\nI make sure to get up, make my bed right away, and open the curtains to let the light in. I think mornings are actually quite nice. I certainly wouldn't want to just lie there feeling sorry for myself—that sets such a bad tone for the whole day, don't you agree? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the mornings are worse at all. I'm doing my absolute best to keep a proper routine and stay productive right from the start. That's the right way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you manage to do them?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I suppose I haven't been doing as much of that lately. But it's not that I don't care, exactly! It's just... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic. \n\nYou see, I used to just love getting out my watercolors, and tending to my tomato plants, and... oh, even the line dancing I used to do. And watching the cardinals out back, they're so beautiful. But lately, they just sort of... sit there. The paints, I mean. And I look out the window and I just can't seem to make myself feel that same spark about it all. \n\nI feel awful even saying it, because I know I should be keeping busy and enjoying my hobbies. It makes me sound so ungrateful, doesn't it? I really am trying to find the joy in things, Doctor, I promise. It's just been a little harder to muster up the energy to care. That's normal though, right? Just a little phase I need to push through?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood has felt low for most of the time, and it didn’t seem to get better, even a little, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, for two weeks straight? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I have my moments where I feel a bit tired or... or flat, like I mentioned with the painting, but it certainly hasn't been an everyday thing, all day long. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around letting myself go. I try very hard to shake it off if I feel a little slump coming on. I'll make a cup of tea or call my neighbor. I wouldn't let it drag on for weeks without trying to fix it. That wouldn't be very responsible of me, would it? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say it's been a constant low like that. I'm doing my best to keep a brave face and keep moving forward. I hope that's the right way to look at it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve completely lost interest in doing your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you have the time and opportunity to do so, and that this has been happening nearly every day for the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose when you put it that way, yes. Goodness knows I have plenty of time on my hands these days. But... well, like I mentioned earlier, the things I always used to love—my watercolors, the tomato plants, the line dancing, and even looking out for the cardinals... they really do just sit there now. \n\nI look at my paints and I tell myself, 'You should go do that, it'll be good for you,' but... I just can't seem to make myself care about them. Not even a little bit. And yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, I suppose it has been like that almost every day for the last few weeks. \n\nI feel so terribly lazy admitting that to you. I really shouldn't be wasting my days like this, should I? I know I need to try harder to keep busy. I'm trying to be better about it, Doctor, I truly am. Is it... is it awful of me to just not have the interest right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt that nothing you do is enjoyable or gives you pleasure, even the things you used to like, most days for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... yes, I suppose we did just touch on that, didn't we? I look at them and I just can't make myself care about them, no matter how hard I try to force a smile and get on with it. \n\nI know I already told you that, I'm so sorry to sound like a broken record. I just want to make sure I'm doing this right. Am I giving you the information you need, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt that nothing you do is enjoyable or gives you pleasure, even the things you used to like, most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel like I'm taking up so much of your time making you ask me the same sort of thing again. I'm so sorry if I'm not being clear enough! I really am trying my best to answer you properly. \n\nBut, yes, it's... it's just as we were saying. I don't mean to sound ungrateful for all the nice things I have in my life, but for the last couple of weeks, it's just been so hard to find the joy in them. That sounds so... well, so gloomy, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to be a bother to anyone by being down in the dumps all the time. I still get up, make my tea, and try to keep busy. Like I said, it's really just the fogginess that's been bothering me. \n\nI try very hard to be cheerful, I really do. You don't think I sound terribly down, do you, Doctor? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm not trying my best to stay positive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your mood has been especially low or worse in the mornings lately?\n\nPatient: Worse in the mornings? Oh, goodness, no, I don't believe so. I always try very hard to start the day off on the right foot, you know? \n\nI make sure to get up, make my bed right away, and open the curtains to let the light in. I think mornings are actually quite nice. I certainly wouldn't want to just lie there feeling sorry for myself—that sets such a bad tone for the whole day, don't you agree? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the mornings are worse at all. I'm doing my absolute best to keep a proper routine and stay productive right from the start. That's the right way to handle things, isn't it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you manage to do them?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I suppose I haven't been doing as much of that lately. But it's not that I don't care, exactly! It's just... well, maybe I haven't been quite as enthusiastic. \n\nYou see, I used to just love getting out my watercolors, and tending to my tomato plants, and... oh, even the line dancing I used to do. And watching the cardinals out back, they're so beautiful. But lately, they just sort of... sit there. The paints, I mean. And I look out the window and I just can't seem to make myself feel that same spark about it all. \n\nI feel awful even saying it, because I know I should be keeping busy and enjoying my hobbies. It makes me sound so ungrateful, doesn't it? I really am trying to find the joy in things, Doctor, I promise. It's just been a little harder to muster up the energy to care. That's normal though, right? Just a little phase I need to push through?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where your mood has felt low for most of the time, and it didn’t seem to get better, even a little, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, for two weeks straight? No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I have my moments where I feel a bit tired or... or flat, like I mentioned with the painting, but it certainly hasn't been an everyday thing, all day long. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around letting myself go. I try very hard to shake it off if I feel a little slump coming on. I'll make a cup of tea or call my neighbor. I wouldn't let it drag on for weeks without trying to fix it. That wouldn't be very responsible of me, would it? \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say it's been a constant low like that. I'm doing my best to keep a brave face and keep moving forward. I hope that's the right way to look at it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve completely lost interest in doing your watercolors or watching the birds, even when you have the time and opportunity to do so, and that this has been happening nearly every day for the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose when you put it that way, yes. Goodness knows I have plenty of time on my hands these days. But... well, like I mentioned earlier, the things I always used to love—my watercolors, the tomato plants, the line dancing, and even looking out for the cardinals... they really do just sit there now. \n\nI look at my paints and I tell myself, 'You should go do that, it'll be good for you,' but... I just can't seem to make myself care about them. Not even a little bit. And yes, if I'm being perfectly honest, I suppose it has been like that almost every day for the last few weeks. \n\nI feel so terribly lazy admitting that to you. I really shouldn't be wasting my days like this, should I? I know I need to try harder to keep busy. I'm trying to be better about it, Doctor, I truly am. Is it... is it awful of me to just not have the interest right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt that nothing you do is enjoyable or gives you pleasure, even the things you used to like, most days for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... yes, I suppose we did just touch on that, didn't we? I hope I'm not making you repeat yourself because I didn't explain it well enough the first time! \n\nBut yes, it's... it's exactly like you said. It sounds so terrible to admit that nothing is enjoyable right now—I really don't want to sound like a gloomy person, because I promise I'm usually not! But just like I was saying a moment ago, all those things I used to just love doing... the watercolors, my tomato plants, the line dancing, and watching the cardinals out back... they all just sit there now. I look at them and I just can't make myself care about them, no matter how hard I try to force a smile and get on with it. \n\nI know I already told you that, I'm so sorry to sound like a broken record. I just want to make sure I'm doing this right. Am I giving you the information you need, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt that nothing you do is enjoyable or gives you pleasure, even the things you used to like, most days for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Oh, dear... I feel like I'm taking up so much of your time making you ask me the same sort of thing again. I'm so sorry if I'm not being clear enough! I really am trying my best to answer you properly. \n\nBut, yes, it's... it's just as we were saying. I don't mean to sound ungrateful for all the nice things I have in my life, but for the last couple of weeks, it's just been so hard to find the joy in them. My watercolors, the tomato plants I was so proud of, the line dancing classes, and even my little cardinals at the feeder... they're all still there, but I just can't seem to muster up the care for them right now. \n\nI know I shouldn't be like this, and I really am trying to snap out of it so I can get back to being my cheerful self. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy, Doctor? I'm answering as honestly as I can, I promise. Is that what you needed to know?"
